<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28139">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28139">46</a> File No. INF26-28139</h3>
<p>Resolution rescinding Resolution 2026-636 passed and adopted on August 6,2026 and authorizing an Amendment to the Agreement for Consultant Services between the City of Tampa and HNTB Corporation regarding Contract 24-D-00034-CSA01 Regional Infrastructure Accelerator in the amount of $999,800; authorizing the Mayor to execute said Amendment on behalf of the City of Tampa; providing for funding thereof.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28139">
<h4 id="financial-28139" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves an amendment to the agreement between the City of Tampa and HNTB Corporation for consulting services in the amount of $999,800 within the Transportation Grants Capital Projects Fund for use by the Multi Modal Infrastructure Financing Opportunities project.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted</dt><dd>$999,800</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$999,800</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Transportation  Grants Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Transportation Division Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Federal Grant - Other Transportation</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Multi Modal Infrastructure Financing Opportunities</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30400.243000.331490.1002794</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue Increase</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Contract Title: 24-D-00034-CSA01 Regional Infrastructure Accelerator a. Address of Project: Citywide b. Number of bidders/submitters: N/A c. Names of bidders/submitters: N/A d. Dollar amount: $999,800.00 e. Why the awardee was selected: In compliance with requirements of Section 287.055, Florida Statues Consultants Competitive Negotiation Act.</li><li>Contractor/Firm: HNTB Corporation</li><li>Business Case: It is necessary to rescind Resolution 2026-636 adopted August 6,2026 to incorporate language in the Amendment referencing certain Federal Execute Orders, as requested by the U.S. Department of Transportation.</li><li>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: HNTB Corporation, the prime contractor, is utilizing no City-certified SLBE firms for subcontracting related to Amendment 1.</li><li>Sustainability and Resiliency: An initial assessment of pending projects across the Tampa Bay Region will be conducted to develop a “long list of projects” (“Long List”) for project pipeline evaluation.</li><li>Community Engagement: Through an iterative stakeholder outreach and consultation process, additional projects for screening may be identified and incorporated into the Long List.</li><li>Project: a. Contract delivery method: This is a Design RFQ project.</li></ol><p>The project provides for maintaining and updating a work plan to ensure effective coordination, communication, and analysis, supporting the City in implementing the Regional Infrastructure Accelerator Project (Project).</p>
<p>Services will include project management and cost controls, including: • Coordinating requirements with the City and project team.</p>
<p>• Establishing and maintaining a cost control system to process, track, and report project costs, including invoicing and payments.</p>
<p>• Preparing invoices with narrative descriptions of project progress and task completion.</p>
<p>• Conducting bi-weekly check-ins with the City and the Tampa Bay Partnership to review project progress.</p>
<p>Amendment 1 provides for an analysis to support planning and investment decisions by identifying how asset concessions and dispositions can enhance land value, attract developers, and align with City transportation and development goals. Services will include, but not be limited to: • Assessing the development potential of City assets.</p>
<p>• Evaluating the role of concessions and dispositions in catalyzing development.</p>
<p>• Evaluating the role of concessions and dispositions in generating new revenue streams.</p>
<p>• Analyzing market conditions and comparable Transit Oriented Development (TOD) and other real estate projects.</p>
<p>• Recommending concession and disposition strategies that align with public policy and economic development, and transportation investment goals. Concession strategies may include, but are not limited to public-private partnerships, asset monetization, and joint development.</p>
<p>b. Placemaking: This project aims to evaluate the strategic use of concessions and dispositions to stimulate development on or generate revenue from undeveloped/underutilized assets owned by the City of Tampa (City).</p>
<p>c. Time, Quality Assurance, Cost Monitoring (Budget) • 300 consecutive calendar days.</p>
<p>• The e-Builder project management software system will be utilized to optimize the monitoring of schedules and costs. This software was recently purchased by the City of Tampa and is a premier system for construction project management.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28139/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28139/r24-d-00034-csa01-ria-rescind-and-amd-r2.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">R24-D-00034-CSA01 RIA RESCIND AND AMD R2.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28139/24-d-00034-csa01-reso-2026-636.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">24-D-00034-CSA01 RESO 2026-636.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28139/24-d-00034-csa01-federal-exec-orders.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">24-D-00034-CSA01 FEDERAL EXEC ORDERS.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28139/24-d-00034-consult-svc-agmt-ntp-execdocs.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">24-D-00034 CONSULT SVC AGMT NTP-EXECDOCS.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28245">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28245">47</a> File No. INF26-28245</h3>
<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 48) Resolution of the City of Tampa, Florida, approving Standard Grant Agreement No. VW507 between the City of Tampa and the State of Florida Department of Environmental Protection for acceptance of grant funds in the amount of $800,000 to purchase solid waste diesel trucks.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28245">
<h4 id="financial-28245" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves the grant agreement between the City and the Florida Department of Environmental Protection and provides $800,000 for the FDEP Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project within the Solid Waste Grants Fund. The City is required to provide a match of $119,215.46 from the Solid Waste Operations Fund for this funding opportunity.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$919,215</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted</dt><dd>$800,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$800,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Fl Dept of Environmental Protection Rev</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41410.239500.334302</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$800,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Transp Equip</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41410.239500.564001</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Increase</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$119,215.46</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Transp Equip</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41400.239500.564001</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa has applied for grant funding in the amount of $800,000 from the State of Florida Department of Environmental Protection for the Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project. The State of Florida Department of Environmental Protection has approved the City of Tampa’s application for funding for the Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project, and the City of Tampa desires to accept the awarded grant.</p>
<p>The grant funds will be used for the purchase of two new Class 8 trucks to replace higher-emitting diesel Class 8 trucks. This Grant Agreement requires the Grantee to complete four tasks.</p>
<p>Task 1, the Grantee will identify the existing eligible diesel trucks that the Grantee proposes to scrap and submit specific information to the Department on Worksheet 1 (Diesel Truck Eligibility Certification) for the Department’s review and approval.</p>
<p>Task 2, the Grantee will purchase and receive delivery of new trucks.</p>
<p>Task 3, the Grantee will complete the scrapping process and submit a completed Worksheet 2 (Certificate of Truck Engine and Chassis Destruction) for each scrapped truck.</p>
<p>Task 4, the Grantee will submit to the Department documentation of the purchase and receipt of the new trucks, including a photo of each new truck, an invoice for each truck, proof of payment for each truck, a Summary Detail Spreadsheet, and a completed and signed Exhibit B Property Reporting Form.</p>
<p>There is a required grantee match of $119,215.46 for a total project cost of $919,215.46.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28245/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28245/reso-and-agreement-no.-vw507.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">RESO AND AGREEMENT NO. VW507.PDF</a></li>
<li class="is-new"><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28245/memo.rogero-110526-regular-council-session-inf26-28245.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">MEMO.ROGERO - 110526 REGULAR COUNCIL SESSION - INF26-28245.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28311">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28311">48</a> File No. INF26-28311</h3>
<p>(Part 2 of 2)(See Item 47) Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2027; approving the transfer, reallocation, and/or appropriation of $919,215.46 within the Solid Waste Grants Fund for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management for the Solid Waste FDEP Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28311">
<h4 id="financial-28311" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution appropriates $800,000 of grant revenue from FDEP within the Solid Waste Grants Fund and realigns $119,215.46 within the Solid Waste Operations Fund for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management for the FDEP Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Funds moved between accounts within the same fund or department; not net new spending."><dt>Reallocated</dt><dd>$919,215</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted</dt><dd>$800,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$800,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Fl Dept of Environmental Protection Rev</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41410.239500.334302</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$800,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Transp Equip</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41410.239500.564001</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Increase</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure-decrease "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$119,215.46</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Transp Equip</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41400.239500.564001</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Decrease</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$119,215.46</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Transp Equip</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41400.239500.564001</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Increase</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa and the Florida Department of Environmental Protection (FDEP) are entering into a Standard Grant Agreement in the amount of $919,215.46 for the Solid Waste FDEP Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management.</p>
<p>The grant provides $800,000 from FDEP for the purchase of two (2) new Class 8 trucks to replace higher-emitting diesel Class 8 trucks. The City is required to provide a match of $119,215.46 for this funding opportunity.</p>
<p>This resolution appropriates $800,000 of grant revenue from FDEP within the Solid Waste Grants Fund and realigns $119,215.46 within the Solid Waste Operations Fund for the FDEP Class 8 Municipal Service Trucks Project.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28311/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28311/fdep-class-8-garbage-truck-project-grant_reso_acm.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">FDEP CLASS 8 GARBAGE TRUCK PROJECT GRANT_RESO_ACM.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28195">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28195">49</a> File No. INF26-28195</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City Council of the City of Tampa, Florida, relating to the Florida Department of Environmental Protection (“FDEP”) State Revolving Fund (“SRF”) program; approving the Water Facilities Plan and Business Plan for the Hillsborough River Reservoir Dam Remediation Project.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-label="Financial impact">
<h4 class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution authorizes the City to submit the Facilities Plan and Business Plan through the Florida Department of Environmental Protection’s State Revolving Fund Program for project financing of $20 million with a 50% principal forgiveness provision, such that one-half of the principal amount of the loan will not be required to be repaid by the City. The interest rate is 0% with a 2% one-time service fee. The City’s water system has sufficient net revenues to service the projected annual debt service for a term of 20 years. The final annual debt service will be calculated upon completion of the financed projects and final closing of the loan. Projected Amount Breakdown: $20,000,000 - Loan $20,000,000 - SUBTOTAL (disbursable amount) $400,000 - 2% one-time service fee $20,400,000 - SUBTOTAL (loan amount) ($10,000,000) - less principal forgiveness $10,400,000 - TOTAL (estimated loan principal amount)</p>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">The proceeds will be deposited in a new fund once the loan has been</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">received for use by the Water Department.</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$20,000,000 - Loan</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$20,000,000 - SUBTOTAL (disbursable amount)</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$400,000 - 2% one-time service fee</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$20,400,000 - SUBTOTAL (loan amount)</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">($10,000,000) - less principal forgiveness</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$10,400,000 - TOTAL (estimated loan principal amount)</li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>To secure a $20 million state loan and grant package for the Hillsborough River Reservoir Dam Remediation project, City Council’s approval of the Water Facilities Plan and Business Plan is required by November 15. Half of the $20 million funding is forgiven as a grant, while the remaining $10 million is an interest-free loan (with a $400,000 service fee) to be repaid using water system revenues, helping cover the project&#39;s total estimated $38.9 million construction cost.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28195/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28195/2026-resolution-and-facilities-plan-and-business-plan-10262026.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2026 RESOLUTION AND FACILITIES PLAN AND BUSINESS PLAN - 10262026.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28195/reso-2026-170-and-srf-loan-application.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">RESO 2026-170 AND SRF LOAN APPLICATION.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28195/facilities-plan-memo-to-director-002.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">FACILITIES PLAN MEMO TO DIRECTOR (002).PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28195/srf-faciliteis-plan-staff-report.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">SRF FACILITEIS PLAN STAFF REPORT.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="finance-committee">Finance Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28190">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28190">50</a> File No. F26-28190</h3>
<p>Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2026; approving the transfer, reallocation, and/or appropriation of $657,515 for the Historic Streetcar Operations within the Streetcar Administration Fund and the General Fund.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28190">
<h4 id="financial-28190" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The FY2026 Adopted Budget included operating expenses to support the operation of the Tampa Historic Streetcar within the Tampa–Ybor Historic Electric Streetcar Special Services District. However, the adopted budget did not include appropriations to cover HART’s overhead expenses. This resolution primarily establishes the necessary budget authority to fund those overhead costs, net of personnel overages and additional advertising revenues.</p>
<p>Pursuant to Section 166.241(8)(a), under certain circumstances a local government within 60 days following the end of the fiscal year may amend a budget for that year.</p>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>This resolution realigns expenditure appropriations in the amount of $200,000 for personnel costs within the General Fund. The changes contained within this resolution represent a realignment of existing appropriations. It has no impact on the General Fund’s FY2026 fiscal year end projected financial condition and requires no use of fund balance.</p>
<p>This resolution also appropriates $1,400,000 within the Fleet Maintenance Operations Fund for year-end operating expenses.</p>
<p>On 10/23/2025, City Council approved R2025-905 authorizing the appropriation and transfer of Solid Waste Operations fund balance of $10,000,000 to the Solid Waste Capital/Construction Fund for the McKay Bay WTE Generator Project. The project is complete and the available project balance of $3,834,191.34 is being returned to fund balance within the Solid Waste Operations Fund.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28233">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28233">52</a> File No. F26-28233</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City Council of the City of Tampa, Florida, superseding all previous resolutions regarding the schedule of wastewater disposal charges for all City of Tampa wastewater customers within the City of Tampa’s service area; adopting new wastewater monthly base charges and wastewater disposal rate charges.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-label="Financial impact">
<h4 class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to the bills of non- City customers. The proposed wastewater consumption rates schedule increases City residents’ rates by 9% and decreases non-City customer rates by 16%. The proposed wastewater base charges schedule reduces the base charges for non-City customers so that non-City customers pay the same base charges as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027. Failure to adopt the proposed wastewater disposal rate and base charges schedules will result in lost wastewater revenues of approximately $12 million annually.</p>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">Failure to adopt the proposed wastewater disposal rate and base</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">charges schedules will result in lost wastewater revenues of approximately $12 million</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">annually.</li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa provides wastewater collection services to customers both within the city and in surrounding areas outside the municipal limits. Recently enacted Chapter 2026 135, Laws of Florida, which takes effect July 1, 2027, amends section 180.191, Florida Statutes, and changes how municipalities may apply surcharges to customers located outside their boundaries. To ensure compliance with these new statutory requirements, and under the authority of Section 26 31 of the City of Tampa Code of Ordinances, the City has determined that updates to its wastewater monthly base charges and disposal rates are necessary.</p>
<p>Additionally, in 2019 the City conducted a comprehensive Rate Study to identify the funding needed to support implementation of the Wastewater Master Plan.</p>
<p>As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to the bills of non-City customers.  The proposed wastewater disposal rates schedule increases City residents’ rates by 9% and decreases non-City customer rates by 16%. The proposed wastewater base charges schedule reduces the base charges for non-City customers so that non-City customers pay the same base charges as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027.  Failure to adopt the proposed wastewater disposal rate and base charges schedules will result in lost wastewater revenues of approximately $12 million annually.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28233/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28233/background-utility-rate-study-final.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">BACKGROUND - UTILITY RATE STUDY - FINAL.PDF</a></li>

<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28233/memo.baird-110526-regular-council-session-onbase-agenda-item-no.-28233-28235-28240.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">MEMO.BAIRD - 110526 REGULAR COUNCIL SESSION - ONBASE AGENDA ITEM NO. 28233 - 28235 - 28240.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28233/water-and-wastewater-extraterritorial-surcharge-council-meeting.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">WATER AND WASTEWATER EXTRATERRITORIAL SURCHARGE COUNCIL MEETING.PPTX</a></li>
</ul>

</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28235">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28235">53</a> File No. F26-28235</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City Council of the City of Tampa, Florida, superseding all previous resolutions regarding the schedule of water usage charges for all City of Tampa water customers within the City of Tampa service area; adopting new water usage rate and monthly base charges; approving a new potable water rate schedule.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-label="Financial impact">
<h4 class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to the bills of non- City customers. The proposed water consumption rates schedule increases City residents’ rates by 9% and decreases non-City customer rates by 16%. The proposed water base charges schedule reduces the base charges for non-City customers so that non-City customers pay the same base charges as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027. Failure to adopt the proposed water consumption rate and base charges schedules will result in lost water revenues of approximately $16 million annually.</p>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">Failure to adopt the proposed water consumption rate and base</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">charges schedules will result in lost water revenues of approximately $16 million annually.</li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa provides potable water service to customers within the City’s service area, which includes customers beyond the City’s municipal boundaries. Chapter 2026-135, Laws of Florida, amends section 180.191, Florida Statutes, (effective July 1, 2027), which modifies a municipality’s authority to impose a surcharge on customers outside of a municipal boundary pursuant to the authority in Section 26-31 of the City of Tampa Code of Ordinances, the City has determined that it is necessary to revise its water monthly base charges and usage rates to comply with section 180.191, Florida Statutes.</p>
<p>As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to the bills of non-City customers.  The proposed water consumption rates schedule increases City residents’ rates by 9% and decreases non-City customer rates by 16%. The proposed water base charges schedule reduces the base charges for non-City customers so that non-City customers pay the same base charges as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027.  Failure to adopt the proposed water consumption rate and base charges schedules will result in lost water revenues of approximately $16 million annually.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28240">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28240">54</a> File No. F26-28240</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City Council of the City of Tampa, Florida, superseding all previous resolutions regarding the schedule of water connection fees, not including reclaimed water rates, for all City of Tampa water customers within the City of Tampa service area.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-label="Financial impact">
<h4 class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to water connection fees paid by non-City customers. The proposed water connection fees schedule eliminates the surcharge so that non-City customers pay the same water connection fees as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027. Failure to adopt the proposed water connection fee schedules will result in lost water revenues of approximately $80,000 annually.</p>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">Failure to adopt the proposed water connection fee schedules will</li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">result in lost water revenues of approximately $80,000 annually.</li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa provides potable water service to customers within the City’s service area, which includes customers beyond the City’s municipal boundaries.  Chapter 2026-135, Laws of Florida, amends section 180.191, Florida Statutes, (effective July 1, 2027), which modifies a municipality’s authority to impose a surcharge on customers outside of a municipal boundary. Pursuant to the authority in Section 26-31 of the City of Tampa Code of Ordinances, the City has determined that it is necessary to revise its water connection fees to comply with section 180.191, Florida Statutes.</p>
<p>As of July 1, 2027, the City is unable to continue adding the 25% surcharge to water connection fees paid by non-City customers. The proposed water connection fees schedule eliminates the surcharge so that non-City customers pay the same water connection fees as City customers. The recommended rate schedules will become effective on July 1, 2027.</p>
<p>Failure to adopt the proposed water connection fee schedules will result in lost water revenues of approximately $80,000 annually.</p>
</details>

<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="development-and-economic-opportunity-committee">Development and Economic Opportunity Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-27671">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-27671">55</a> File No. DEO26-27671</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the Execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS Grant Agreement between the City of Tampa and Metropolitan Charities, Inc., for the Provision of Housing and other Supportive Services to Persons with Acquired Immune Deficiency Syndrome (AIDS) or related diseases and their affected resident family members under the Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS Grant Program of the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development in an amount not to exceed $675,000.00 over a three-year term.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>

Subrecipient shall be reimbursed for Supportive Services in an amount not to exceed $225,000 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $675,000 from the Housing and Community Development Department’s HOPWA Grant Fund. Funds in the amount of $225,000 are available within the 2027 HOPWA grant fund. FY2028 and FY2029 funding is contingent upon available 2028 and 2029 HOPWA grant funds.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$225,000</dd></div></dl>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-27674">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-27674">57</a> File No. DEO26-27674</h3>
<p>Resolution approving a Software Service and Licensing Agreement between the City of Tampa and City Wise Software LLC (“City Wise”) for a housing software platform to list available rental properties within the City of Tampa.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The Housing and Community Development (HCD) Department assists residents in locating affordable rental housing and connecting them with available housing resources. Currently, rental housing information is available through various sources.  However, those sources are not always current, may not allow users to easily filter available units based on income eligibility or other housing considerations, and may include properties located outside the city limits of Tampa.</p>
<p>The proposed agreement with City Wise Software LLC will provide a web-based rental housing portal accessible through the City&#39;s HCD website. The portal will consolidate rental listings from multiple sources into one location and provide search tools to help residents identify available housing, including units that may be affordable to income-qualified households and households utilizing housing vouchers.</p>
<p>CityWise will host, maintain, support, and update the rental housing portal. The platform will provide direct links and contact information for available rental listings and will integrate with commonly used property-management systems to assist with keeping listing information current. CityWise will also review listings included on the site.</p>
<p>CityWise currently operates in more than 20 cities across the country, including St.</p>
<p>Petersburg, Florida, as well as other municipalities of comparable size.</p>
<p>The portal will serve as an additional resource for residents and HCD staff and is intended to improve access to rental housing information while reducing the time required to search multiple sources for available units.</p>
<p>There is no fiscal impact to the City associated with this agreement. CityWise will provide the platform at no licensing or subscription cost to the City.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28141">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28141">61</a> File No. DEO26-28141-Resoluti</h3>
<p>Resolution approving an Interlocal Agreement between the City of Tampa and Hillsborough County for the performance of certain workshops and entrepreneur services by Hillsborough County Entrepreneur Collaborative Center for an amount not to exceed $84,150 during the 2027 fiscal year.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28141">
<h4 id="financial-28141" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">As consideration for the performance of the services by the County referenced herein, the City shall reimburse the County for all allowable costs in an amount not to exceed Eighty-Four Thousand One Hundred Fifty Dollars ($84,150.00) from Development and Economic Opportunity within the General Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$84,150</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$84,150</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Hillsborough County Small Business Contribution</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.582505</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa has partnered with Hillsborough County’s Entrepreneur Collaborative Center (ECC) since 2018 to accomplish economic development goals. The requested $84,150.00 funding for FY2027 will assist the ECC in providing small business workshops, training, business consulting, and other assistance to existing and start-up small businesses in the City of Tampa.</p>
<p>The Hillsborough Entrepreneur Collaborative Center has assisted small businesses as follows (through Q3-FY2026): 12 General Workshops with 313 Attendees 2 Business Plan Workshop Series with 51 Attendees 2 Business Plan in a Day Consulting with 34 Attendees 1 Entrepreneur Training with 8 Attendees</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>

<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28141/hceccreso.agreement3.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">HCECCRESO.AGREEMENT(3).PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28161">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28161">62</a> File No. DEO26-28161</h3>
<p>Resolution approving an Agreement between the City of Tampa and the Tampa Bay Convention and Visitors Bureau, Inc. (dba Visit Tampa Bay) on behalf of the Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office for the performance of certain protocol and diplomatic services by the Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office for an amount not to exceed $48,000 during the 2027 Fiscal Year.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28161">
<h4 id="financial-28161" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">For its performance under this Agreement, the City shall pay to the Recipient an amount not to exceed $48,000 from Development and Economic Opportunity within the General Fund, payable for services as set forth in Exhibit A, as full compensation for all work done and materials furnished, unless otherwise authorized by the parties in a written modification agreement. Payments will be made in accordance with the procedures specified in Exhibit B, Method of Payment, and Exhibit C, Request for Payment Form, both of which are attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$48,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$48,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">The Tampa Bay Convention and Visitors Bureau, Inc., (DBA Visit Tampa Bay) on Behalf of The Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">, (DBA Visit Tampa Bay) on Behalf of The Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.582500</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa and Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office have partnered since 2012. The agency will be utilizing the FY2027 $48,000 funding to support their work with consuls, ambassadors and diplomatic trade commissioners in enhancing investment and trade opportunities within the Tampa Bay region. Staff from the Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office support top public officials, such as the Mayor, City Council Chair, and the Chair of the County Commission, in communications with high-level delegations of international business and government organizations. The Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office also partners with Tampa Sister Cities, USF World, Bi-National Chambers, and the United States Global Leadership as an advisory member for the State.</p>
<p>The Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office has facilitated outbound missions to approximately 19 countries in the past five years. In FY2026 (Q1-Q3), the Tampa Bay Trade &amp; Protocol Office assisted as follows: Consular Corps Meetings: 10 Inbound Dignitary Visits: 5 Outbound Delegations: 1 Protocol Training: 2 Bi-National Meetings: 2</p>
</details>

</ul>

<p>Resolution approving an agreement between the City of Tampa and Prospera Initiatives, Inc. for the performance of certain bilingual consulting and technical assistance services for small businesses for an amount not to exceed $80,000.00 during the 2027 Fiscal Year.</p>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28165">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28165">65</a> File No. DEO26-28165</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a release of easement in favor of the City of Tampa for drainage and stormwater facilities located in the vicinity of 4201 W. Gandy Boulevard, Tampa, Florida, and approving the acceptance of a replacement easement for stormwater drainage and wastewater purposes from Bank of America, National Association, successor in interest to NationsBank, National Association, to the City of Tampa for the installation, construction, repair, operation, maintenance and replacement of drainage facilities and for the installation, construction, repair, operation, maintenance and replacement of wastewater facilities in the vicinity of 4201 W. Gandy Boulevard, Tampa, Florida.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City acquired a grant of easement for stormwater purposes in 1974 for the property located in the vicinity of 4201 W. Gandy Boulevard. The City wishes to replace the original easement with a new easement for both stormwater drainage and wastewater purposes. The owner has agreed to allow the release of the original easement to avoid any conflict and/or confusion between the original easement and the new grant of easement.  The footprint of the new easement is the same as the original easement.</p>
</details>

<p>Resolution approving an agreement between the City of Tampa and Tampa Bay Wave Inc. for the performance of certain technical assistance for innovation-based or technology industries services for an amount not to exceed $100,000.00 during the 2027 Fiscal Year.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>

<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">For its performance, under this Agreement, the City shall pay to the Recipient an amount not to exceed $100,000.00 from the Development and Economic Opportunity Department within the General Fund, payable for services set forth in Exhibit A, as full compensation for all work done and materials furnished, unless otherwise authorized by the City in a written modification agreement. Payments will be made in accordance with the procedures specified in Exhibit B, Method of Payment, and Exhibit C, Request for Payment Form, both of which are attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference. The Recipient shall submit program performance report(s) in accordance with Exhibit D, Program Performance Report, attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference, with all payment requests.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$100,000</dd></div></dl>

<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$100,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Tampa Bay Wave Contribution</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.582531</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>

<p>The City of Tampa has partnered with Tampa Bay Wave since 2013 in serving our business community.  Tampa Bay Wave will be utilizing the $100,000 FY2027 funding to provide zero‑equity, vertical-specific acceleration programs—covering areas like FinTech, HealthTech, Cybersecurity, and Tech|X—complete with mentorship, investor introductions, and hands-on support to help founders launch and scale tech ventures. Tampa Bay Wave’s technical assistance includes direct business counseling, ongoing training, educational programs/ workshops and business planning services.</p>
<p>In FY2026 (Q1-Q3), Tampa Bay Wave assisted our technology and innovation based businesses as follows: Capital Raised: $258,160,000.00 New Companies: 26 New Jobs: 460 New Jobs in City of Tampa Limits: 16 Start-up Business Consulting: 46 Strategic Connections: 629 Effort to recruit companies to Tampa Bay: 11</p>

<p>Resolution approving the execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a Private Dock Agreement between the City of Tampa and Daryl Sullivan for the construction and operation of certain dock structures and facilities over or on a portion of submerged sovereign lands owned and held by the City in the vicinity of 321 West Hanlon Street, Tampa, Florida.</p>

<p>The owner has obtained Minor Work Permit No. 83050 from the Environmental Protection Commission for construction of certain dock structures and facilities on the City’s submerged sovereign land within the Hillsborough River. The Private Dock Agreement will incorporate the EPC permit, which includes the existing rip rap and a wooden seawall on the City’s submerged sovereign land.</p>

<p>Resolution approving the execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a HOME Investment Partnerships Grant Agreement between the City of Tampa and AIDS Service Association of Pinellas, Inc. d/b/a EPIC, for the provision of tenant-based rental assistance to individuals and families experiencing homelessness under the HOME Investment Partnerships Program of the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development, in an amount not to exceed $1,275,000.00 over a three-year term.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28237">
<h4 id="financial-28237" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">The City desires to enter into an Agreement with Aids Service Association of Pinellas, Inc. D/B/A EPIC. The Subrecipient shall be reimbursed for Tenant Based Rental Assistance in an amount not to exceed $425,000 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $1,275,000 from the Housing and Community Development Department’s HOME Grant Fund. Funds in the amount of $425,000 are available within the 2027 HOME grant fund. FY2028 and FY2029 funding is contingent upon available 2028 and 2029 HOME grant funds.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$425,000</dd></div></dl>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__future">Plus <strong>$850,000</strong> in future-year amounts subject to annual Council approval.</p>

<p>Aids Service Association of Pinellas, Inc. D/B/A EPIC has partnered with the City to provide rental assistance services and related supportive services to individuals and families of low- or moderate-income experiencing homelessness within the City of Tampa.</p>
<p>Staff is requesting City Council’s consideration and approval of the attached agreement and resolution to allow for the execution of the Agreement that will allow for Aids Service Association of Pinellas, Inc. D/B/A EPIC to provide Tenant-Based Rental Assistance.  The Housing and Community Development Department will monitor the services to ensure eligible individuals and families are being served.</p>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28238">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28238">70</a> File No. DEO26-28238</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the Execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS Grant Agreement between the City of Tampa and BayCare Behavioral Health, Inc., for the Provision of Housing and other Supportive Services to Persons with Acquired Immune Deficiency Syndrome (AIDS) or related diseases and their affected resident family members under the Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS Grant Program of the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development in an amount not to exceed $840,120.00 over a three-year term.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28238">
<h4 id="financial-28238" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves an Agreement between the City and BayCare Behavioral Health, Inc., for the provision of services in support of Persons with Acquired Immune Deficiency Syndrome (AIDS) under the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development HOPWA program in an amount of $280,040 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $840,120. Subrecipient shall be reimbursed for Permanent-Facility Based Housing assistance in an amount not to exceed $280,040 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $840,120 from the Housing and Community Development Department’s HOPWA Grant Fund. Funds in the amount of $280,040 are available within the 2027 HOPWA grant fund. FY2028 and FY2029 funding is contingent upon available 2028 and 2029 HOPWA grant funds.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$280,040</dd></div></dl>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__future">Plus <strong>$560,080</strong> in future-year amounts subject to annual Council approval.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$280,040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 27</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 12220.218800.534007.1003022</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$280,040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 28</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY28 · 12220.218800.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$280,040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 29</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY29 · 12220.218800.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>BayCare Behavioral Health, Inc. was awarded a 3-year HOPWA Agreement to provide Permanent-Facility Based Housing assistance for persons with HIV/AIDS and their affected resident family members in order to create housing stability and reduce the risk of homelessness in Pasco and Hernando Counties.</p>
</details>

<p>Resolution approving the execution by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk of a HOME Investment Partnerships Grant Agreement between the City of Tampa and Survivor Ventures, Inc., for the provision of tenant-based rental assistance to individuals and families experiencing homelessness under the HOME Investment Partnerships Program of the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development, in an amount not to exceed $525,000.00 over a three-year term.</p>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28320">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28320">72</a> File No. DEO26-28320</h3>
<p>Resolution approving a ground lease agreement, including exhibits, between the City of Tampa and Rome Yards Phase One, LLC, for phase 1 of the development commonly referred to as “Rome Yard”; affecting approximately 4.709 acres of City owned land generally located South of Columbus Drive, West of the Hillsborough River, East of Rome Avenue and North of Spruce Street in the West Tampa community redevelopment area; authorizing the execution thereof and of exhibits by the Mayor and attestation by the City Clerk.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>This resolution approves a Ground Lease Agreement between the City of Tampa and Rome Yards Phase One, LLC for Phase 1 of the Rome Yard redevelopment project in the West Tampa Community Redevelopment Area. The lease covers approximately 4.709 acres of City-owned property located near the Hillsborough River, Rome Avenue, Columbus Drive, and Spruce Street.</p>
</details>

<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="mobility-and-asset-management-committee">Mobility and Asset Management Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-27657">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-27657">73</a> File No. MAM26-27657</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the renewal of an agreement between the City of Tampa and Quality Roofing, Inc., without a price escalation, for the furnishing of Roofing Services in the estimated amount of $2,000,000 for use by the Logistics &amp; Asset Management Department/Facility Management Division.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-label="Financial impact">
<h4 class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution renews the Agreement between the City and Quality Roofing, Inc. for Roofing Services and provides an estimated amount of $2,000,000 over a 12- month term, subject to annual appropriation, for use by the Logistics &amp; Asset Management Department/Facility Management Division and shall be controlled by requisition.</p>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source">$2,000,000 FY2027 (Estimated Expenditure) controlled by requisition</li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>It is recommended that the City renew its existing award with Quality Roofing, Inc. for the provision of Roofing Services for use by the Logistics and Asset Management Department/Facilities Division for an additional one-year term.  The existing award was made June 2023.  This award is in its second renewal term which was extended to November 30, 2026, per the bid document Section 4.14.1 Supplemental Unilateral Renewal Periods.</p>
<p>Quality Roofing, Inc. provides excellent service, quality of product and performance. The Logistics and Asset Management Department/Facilities Division has reviewed the prices and feels that it is in the best interest of the City to renew the award for an additional one-year period.  Quality Roofing, Inc. has agreed to renew Roofing Services without a price escalation.</p>
<p>Equal Business Opportunity Statement: Pursuant to the review by the Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Division, this one-year renewal contract is in compliance.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/27657/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/27657/23-p-00386_backup_docs.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">23-P-00386_BACKUP_DOCS.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/27657/23-p-00386_roofing_services_reso.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">23-P-00386_ROOFING_SERVICES_RESO.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>

<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28094">74</a> File No. MAM26-28094</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City of Tampa, Florida approving a Sale Terms &amp; Conditions Agreement between the City of Tampa and Carlson Preferred Solutions, LLC for the standardization of Carlson GPS, Survey Equipment, Data Collectors and Software Products and Services in the estimated amount of $98,777.12 for use by the Mobility Department.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28094">
<h4 id="financial-28094" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">That this Resolution approves said Agreement between the City of Tampa and Carlson Preferred Solutions, LLC in an estimated amount of $98,777.12 for use by the Mobility Department within the Local Option Gas Tax Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$98,777</dd></div></dl>

<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$98,777.12</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Local Option Gas Tax</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">TSS - Transportation Division</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Computers -  Bulk Purchases Hardware/Software</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 10200.243100.552007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>It is recommended that the City of Tampa enter into an agreement with Carlson Preferred Solutions, LLC for Carlson GPS, Survey Equipment, Data Collectors and Software Products and Services for use by Mobility Department. The quote made part of the Agreement is for the purchase of a 12-month software maintenance plan, and equipment with software licenses which are for a perpetual term.</p>
<p>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: There was no EBO participation, as this purchase is being made under a standardization.</p>

<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28230">76</a> File No. DA26-28230</h3>
<p>Resolution setting public hearings on November 19, 2026 at 1:30 p.m., or as soon thereafter, and December 3, 2026 at 10:30 a.m., or as soon thereafter, in City Council Chambers, 315 East Kennedy Boulevard, 3rd Floor, Tampa, Florida, 33602, relating to the development agreement between the City of Tampa and Clonts Properties, Inc. relating to the property located at 3812 and 3816 Temple Street, Tampa, Florida 33619; providing for two notices of intent to consider said agreement.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>Developer desires to re-develop the Property as a new multi-family affordable housing development, consisting of 14  residential units which shall be affordable. Developer is desiring to schedule the public hearings to consider the Development Agreement.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28431">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28431">77</a> File No. PH26-27662</h3>
<p>Staff to bring a resolution pursuant to the pending ordinance doctrine, to: declare the City’s intent to adopt amendments to the Tampa Comprehensive Plan, Land Development Code and City Code related to data centers, and direct staff to prepare and submit proposed amendments to City Council; direct staff to immediately cease accepting and processing land development applications for data centers; direct staff to prepare an interim development control (moratorium) ordinance on data centers so that staff can explore and develop Comprehensive plan amendments, Land Development Code, and City Code language relating to all aspects of the regulation of data centers; scheduled public hearings on the moratorium ordinance for December 3, 2026 at 5:01 p.m. and January 7, 2027 at 10:00 a.m.; and direct the Clerk to advertise the public hearings for the moratorium ordinance.</p>
</article>
</section>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28318">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28318">79</a> File No. E2026-8</h3>
<p>CH 22 (UNAN; Carlson absent) - An Ordinance of the City of Tampa, Florida, amending the City of Tampa Code of Ordinances, Chapter 22, Article III, Division 3, Section 22-308, “trees, shrubbery, etc., obstructing vision of drivers of motor vehicles declared in violation.”.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>Whereas, the Legislature of the State of Florida has, in Chapter 166, Florida Statutes, Municipalities, conferred upon local governments the authority to adopt regulations designed to promote the public health, safety, and general welfare of its citizenry; and the City of Tampa has determined that the following amendment to Chapter 22, Code of Ordinances is necessary to promote efficient administration; and the City Council of the City of Tampa has determined that the following amendment promotes and protects the general health, safety and welfare of the residents of the City of Tampa; and a duly noticed public hearing as required by law was held by the City Council of the City of Tampa, at which public hearing all residents and interested persons were given an opportunity to be heard.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28356">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28356">80</a> File No. E2026-8</h3>
<p>CH 26.5 (Hurtak no) - An Ordinance of the City of Tampa, Florida, deleting, In Its entirety, the City of Tampa Code of Ordinances, Articles I, II and III of Chapter 26.5, Equal Business Opportunity Program; adopting revised Articles I, II, and III of Chapter 26.5, renaming Equal Business Opportunity to Strategic Business Development.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The Legislature of the State of Florida has, in Chapter 166, Florida Statutes, Municipalities, conferred upon local governments the authority to adopt regulations designed to promote the public health, safety, and general welfare of its citizenry.  Tampa City Council has determined that it is in the public interest to amend Articles I, II, and III of Chapter 26.5, Equal Business Opportunity Program, to promote efficient administration and update the provisions of the Strategic Business Development (f/k/a Equal Business Opportunity Program) to reflect current practices.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28357">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28357">81</a> File No. DA26-26530</h3>
<p>(UNAN) - An Ordinance granting to Robles Park, LLC, Robles Park Village Development Group, LLC, RPV Parcel D, L.P., its successors and assigns, a non-exclusive franchise to use a portion of North Avon Avenue, in the City of Tampa to construct, access and maintain stormwater improvements and prescribing the terms and conditions under which said non-exclusive franchise may be exercised.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28357">
<h4 id="financial-28357" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This ordinance approves an agreement between the City of Tampa and Robles Park, LLC, Robles Park Village Development Group, LLC, and Open Space Acquisitions, LLC to use a portion of the right of way located within North Avon Avenue to construct, access, and maintain Stormwater Improvements. The City will collect a franchise fee in the amount of $2,500 in FY2027, with a 3% annual increase over a 20 year period for a total of $67,175.94. These funds will be collected by the Stormwater Department and deposited within the Stormwater Service Assessment Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted (FY27)</dt><dd>$2,500</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$2,500</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Stormwater Service Assessment Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">TSS - Stormwater Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Franchise Fees-Other</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 10700.234300.323900</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City and Robles Park, LLC, Robles Park Village Development Group, LLC, and Open Space Acquisitions, LLC (collectively, the “Developer”) entered into a Development Agreement, dated September 5, 2024, regarding the development of property located on N.</p>
<p>Central Avenue, Tampa, Fl (“Development Agreement”).  As part of the development, a private stormwater pipe will be crossing North Avon Avenue, which is a public right of way.</p>
<p>The Development Agreement requires that the Developer obtain a franchise from the City in order to allow the stormwater pipe to cross the right of way.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28357/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28357/robles-park-ordinance-franchise-9.18.26.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">ROBLES PARK ORDINANCE- FRANCHISE 9.18.26.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28357/robles-park-business-impact-estimate.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">ROBLES PARK BUSINESS IMPACT ESTIMATE.PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
</section>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28168">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28168">82</a> File No. REZ-26-60</h3>
<p>REZ-26-60 (UNAN) - An ordinance rezoning property in the general vicinity of 1605 North Waters Court, in the city of Tampa, Florida, and more particularly described in Section 1, from zoning district classification(s) YC-2 [Ybor City Historic District (Residential)] to YC-9 [Ybor City Historic District site planned controlled (Residential Multiple Family)].</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-land-use" aria-labelledby="land-use-28168">
<h4 id="land-use-28168" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Land use details</h4>
<dl class="agenda-item-land-use__facts"><div class="agenda-item-land-use__fact"><dt>Future land use</dt><dd>Urban Mixed Use-60</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-land-use__fact"><dt>Neighborhood associations</dt><dd>Historic Ybor Neighborhood Civic Association, Inc</dd><dd>Ybor City Management LLC</dd><dd>David Bailey</dd><dd>Hyde Park Spanishtown Creek Civic Association</dd><dd>Bowman Heights</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers-heading">Waivers requested</h5><ol class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers"><li>Section 27-283.14: To reduce the required number of loading berths from 4 to 2.</li><li>Section 27-283.15: To allow two 12’x30’ loading spaces.</li></ol>
<p class="agenda-item-land-use__findings"><strong>Staff findings:</strong> The Development Review and Compliance staff has reviewed the petition and finds the request INCONSISTENT with the applicable City of Tampa Land Development Regulations. See findings from Transportation related to the numbers and size of the loading berths in the report below. See recommendation of approval from the Barrio Latino Commission in the report below. Should it be the pleasure of City Council to approve the application and waivers listed above, further modifications to the site plan must be completed by the applicant between 1st and 2nd Reading of Ordinance as stated on the revision sheet. These revisions will not resolve the issues of Transportation’s inconsistent finding.</p>
</section>
</details>

<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28168/rez-26-0000060-affidavit-of-compliance.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">REZ-26-0000060 AFFIDAVIT OF COMPLIANCE.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-05/meeting-2938/28168/rez-26-60-staff-report-final...pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">REZ-26-60 STAFF REPORT FINAL...PDF</a></li>

<p>(UNAN) - An ordinance rezoning property in the general vicinity of 3209 North 22nd Street, in the city of Tampa, Florida, and more particularly described in Section 1, from zoning district classification(s) CG (Commercial General) to PD [Planned Development (Pharmacy, and Retail Sales, Convenience Goods &amp; Shopper’s Goods)].</p>

<h5 class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers-heading">Waivers requested</h5><ol class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers"><li>Section 27-283.7: To reduce the required parking from 9 to 1 (89% reduction).</li><li>Section 27-284.3.3: To reduce the use-to-use buffer along the eastern property line from 15’ with a 6’ masonry wall to 2’ with a 6’ high fence.</li><li>Section 27-284.3.3: To reduce the use-to-use buffer along 22nd Street to all adjacent rights of way from 8’ to 0’.</li><li>Section 27-240(e)(2)c.5: To reduce the front yard setback to allow a 0-foot setback instead of the 10’ – 12’ range required due to existing building.</li><li>Section 27-284.4: Waiver to pay into the tree trust fund for required trees not planted on site.</li></ol>
<p class="agenda-item-land-use__findings"><strong>Staff findings:</strong> The Development Review and Compliance staff has reviewed the petition and finds the request INCONSISTENT with the applicable City of Tampa Land Development Regulations. Please refer to Transportation comments for overall inconsistency finding. If City Council approves the application and waivers requested, the modifications to the site plan as shown on the submitted revision sheet must be completed between first and second reading. Revisions will not resolve Transportation’s finding of inconsistent.</p>
</section>

<p>(UNAN) - An ordinance rezoning property in the general vicinity of 1728 East 2nd Avenue, 1704, 1720, and 1722 East 3rd Avenue, in the city of Tampa, Florida, and more particularly described in Section 1, from zoning district classification(s) YC-6 (Ybor City Historic District Community Commercial) and YC-9 (Ybor City site planned controlled) to YC-9 [Ybor City Historic District site planned controlled (Retail Sales, Shoppers’ Goods, All CG Uses)].</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-land-use" aria-labelledby="land-use-28175">
<h4 id="land-use-28175" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Land use details</h4>
<dl class="agenda-item-land-use__facts"><div class="agenda-item-land-use__fact"><dt>Future land use</dt><dd>General Mixed Use-24</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-land-use__fact"><dt>Neighborhood associations</dt><dd>Historic Ybor Neighborhood Civic Association, Inc</dd><dd>Ybor City Management LLC</dd><dd>David Bailey</dd><dd>Hyde Park Spanishtown Creek Civic Association</dd><dd>Bowman Heights</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers-heading">Waivers requested</h5><ol class="agenda-item-land-use__waivers"><li>Section 27-178: To reduce the required number of overall parking spaces in the YC zoning district from 86 required to 46 provided (47% reduction).</li><li>Section 27-283.7: To reduce the required backup width for standard 90-degree parking spaces and compact 90-degree parking spaces from 7 feet and 6 feet, respectively, to 4 feet.</li></ol>
<p class="agenda-item-land-use__findings"><strong>Staff findings:</strong> The Development Review and Compliance staff has reviewed the petition and finds the request INCONSISTENT with the applicable City of Tampa Land Development Regulations. See findings from Transportation in the report below related to the requested reduction in required parking and the requested reduction in required backup width. Should it be the pleasure of City Council to approve the application and waivers listed above, further modifications to the site plan must be completed by the applicant between 1st and 2nd Reading of Ordinance as stated on the revision sheet. These revisions will not resolve the issues of Transportation’s inconsistent finding.</p>
</section>
</details>
